Surah: 49. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/r2/surah_map.md =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not use tools, delegate, browse or inspect files.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the
surah, and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear.
A member need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah,
not in one word. Say plainly when a member is a rare sense or is attested only inside a fixed expression, and
keep it. When the dictionary itself joins two of the surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is
the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at the level of its objects, their parts and their
operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into another's more abstract function unless nothing
concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test, however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain.
2. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: which chains it opens, advances or completes, and what its words add to
   each.
3. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain, one line each:
   its name and why.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s049/surah.r2/text.md =====
# Surah 49

- 49:1 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا تُقَدِّمُوا۟ بَيْنَ يَدَىِ ٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ ۖ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌۭ
- 49:2 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا تَرْفَعُوٓا۟ أَصْوَٰتَكُمْ فَوْقَ صَوْتِ ٱلنَّبِىِّ وَلَا تَجْهَرُوا۟ لَهُۥ بِٱلْقَوْلِ كَجَهْرِ بَعْضِكُمْ لِبَعْضٍ أَن تَحْبَطَ أَعْمَٰلُكُمْ وَأَنتُمْ لَا تَشْعُرُونَ
- 49:3 إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَغُضُّونَ أَصْوَٰتَهُمْ عِندَ رَسُولِ ٱللَّهِ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ ٱمْتَحَنَ ٱللَّهُ قُلُوبَهُمْ لِلتَّقْوَىٰ ۚ لَهُم مَّغْفِرَةٌۭ وَأَجْرٌ عَظِيمٌ
- 49:4 إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يُنَادُونَكَ مِن وَرَآءِ ٱلْحُجُرَٰتِ أَكْثَرُهُمْ لَا يَعْقِلُونَ
- 49:5 وَلَوْ أَنَّهُمْ صَبَرُوا۟ حَتَّىٰ تَخْرُجَ إِلَيْهِمْ لَكَانَ خَيْرًۭا لَّهُمْ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ
- 49:6 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِن جَآءَكُمْ فَاسِقٌۢ بِنَبَإٍۢ فَتَبَيَّنُوٓا۟ أَن تُصِيبُوا۟ قَوْمًۢا بِجَهَٰلَةٍۢ فَتُصْبِحُوا۟ عَلَىٰ مَا فَعَلْتُمْ نَٰدِمِينَ
- 49:7 وَٱعْلَمُوٓا۟ أَنَّ فِيكُمْ رَسُولَ ٱللَّهِ ۚ لَوْ يُطِيعُكُمْ فِى كَثِيرٍۢ مِّنَ ٱلْأَمْرِ لَعَنِتُّمْ وَلَٰكِنَّ ٱللَّهَ حَبَّبَ إِلَيْكُمُ ٱلْإِيمَٰنَ وَزَيَّنَهُۥ فِى قُلُوبِكُمْ وَكَرَّهَ إِلَيْكُمُ ٱلْكُفْرَ وَٱلْفُسُوقَ وَٱلْعِصْيَانَ ۚ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلرَّٰشِدُونَ
- 49:8 فَضْلًۭا مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ وَنِعْمَةًۭ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ عَلِيمٌ حَكِيمٌۭ
- 49:9 وَإِن طَآئِفَتَانِ مِنَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ ٱقْتَتَلُوا۟ فَأَصْلِحُوا۟ بَيْنَهُمَا ۖ فَإِنۢ بَغَتْ إِحْدَىٰهُمَا عَلَى ٱلْأُخْرَىٰ فَقَٰتِلُوا۟ ٱلَّتِى تَبْغِى حَتَّىٰ تَفِىٓءَ إِلَىٰٓ أَمْرِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ فَإِن فَآءَتْ فَأَصْلِحُوا۟ بَيْنَهُمَا بِٱلْعَدْلِ وَأَقْسِطُوٓا۟ ۖ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُقْسِطِينَ
- 49:10 إِنَّمَا ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ إِخْوَةٌۭ فَأَصْلِحُوا۟ بَيْنَ أَخَوَيْكُمْ ۚ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُرْحَمُونَ
- 49:11 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا يَسْخَرْ قَوْمٌۭ مِّن قَوْمٍ عَسَىٰٓ أَن يَكُونُوا۟ خَيْرًۭا مِّنْهُمْ وَلَا نِسَآءٌۭ مِّن نِّسَآءٍ عَسَىٰٓ أَن يَكُنَّ خَيْرًۭا مِّنْهُنَّ ۖ وَلَا تَلْمِزُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَكُمْ وَلَا تَنَابَزُوا۟ بِٱلْأَلْقَٰبِ ۖ بِئْسَ ٱلِٱسْمُ ٱلْفُسُوقُ بَعْدَ ٱلْإِيمَٰنِ ۚ وَمَن لَّمْ يَتُبْ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلظَّٰلِمُونَ
- 49:12 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ ٱجْتَنِبُوا۟ كَثِيرًۭا مِّنَ ٱلظَّنِّ إِنَّ بَعْضَ ٱلظَّنِّ إِثْمٌۭ ۖ وَلَا تَجَسَّسُوا۟ وَلَا يَغْتَب بَّعْضُكُم بَعْضًا ۚ أَيُحِبُّ أَحَدُكُمْ أَن يَأْكُلَ لَحْمَ أَخِيهِ مَيْتًۭا فَكَرِهْتُمُوهُ ۚ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ تَوَّابٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ
- 49:13 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ إِنَّا خَلَقْنَٰكُم مِّن ذَكَرٍۢ وَأُنثَىٰ وَجَعَلْنَٰكُمْ شُعُوبًۭا وَقَبَآئِلَ لِتَعَارَفُوٓا۟ ۚ إِنَّ أَكْرَمَكُمْ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ أَتْقَىٰكُمْ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلِيمٌ خَبِيرٌۭ
- 49:14 ۞ قَالَتِ ٱلْأَعْرَابُ ءَامَنَّا ۖ قُل لَّمْ تُؤْمِنُوا۟ وَلَٰكِن قُولُوٓا۟ أَسْلَمْنَا وَلَمَّا يَدْخُلِ ٱلْإِيمَٰنُ فِى قُلُوبِكُمْ ۖ وَإِن تُطِيعُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥ لَا يَلِتْكُم مِّنْ أَعْمَٰلِكُمْ شَيْـًٔا ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌ
- 49:15 إِنَّمَا ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ ثُمَّ لَمْ يَرْتَابُوا۟ وَجَٰهَدُوا۟ بِأَمْوَٰلِهِمْ وَأَنفُسِهِمْ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلصَّٰدِقُونَ
- 49:16 قُلْ أَتُعَلِّمُونَ ٱللَّهَ بِدِينِكُمْ وَٱللَّهُ يَعْلَمُ مَا فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَمَا فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ بِكُلِّ شَىْءٍ عَلِيمٌۭ
- 49:17 يَمُنُّونَ عَلَيْكَ أَنْ أَسْلَمُوا۟ ۖ قُل لَّا تَمُنُّوا۟ عَلَىَّ إِسْلَٰمَكُم ۖ بَلِ ٱللَّهُ يَمُنُّ عَلَيْكُمْ أَنْ هَدَىٰكُمْ لِلْإِيمَٰنِ إِن كُنتُمْ صَٰدِقِينَ
- 49:18 إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَعْلَمُ غَيْبَ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ ۚ وَٱللَّهُ بَصِيرٌۢ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ


===== _commentary/v16/work/s049/surah.r2/dictionary.md =====
# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

## ء م ن (root_000054): 49:1 ءَامَنُوا۟, 49:2 ءَامَنُوا۟, 49:6 ءَامَنُوٓا۟, 49:7 ٱلْإِيمَٰنَ, 49:9 ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ, 49:10 ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ, 49:11 ءَامَنُوا۟, 49:11 ٱلْإِيمَٰنِ, 49:12 ءَامَنُوا۟, 49:14 ءَامَنَّا, 49:14 تُؤْمِنُوا۟, 49:14 ٱلْإِيمَٰنُ, 49:15 ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ, 49:15 ءَامَنُوا۟, 49:17 لِلْإِيمَٰنِ

- **B001** guven ve guvenilirlik — ihanetin karsiti olan guvenilirlik ve emanet edilen sey · korkunun kalkmasi ve ic yatiskinligi · guven, eminlik ve yatiskinlik hali · guven verme, guven hali veya guvenceye birakilan sey · guven icinde olmak ve korkusu kalkmak · birini guven icine almak ve ona guven saglamak · guven icinde duruma gelmek · kendisine guvenilen emin kisi · emanet edilen veya kendisine guvenilen kisi · guven icinde olan, emin · guvenilir veya emanet edilebilir olan · kendisine bir sey emanet edilen kisi · guven icinde, tehlikeden uzak ve yatiskin · insanlarin zararindan korkmadigi guvenilir kimse · herkese guvenen ve duydugunu dogru sayan kisi · kisinin en degerli ve icinin yatistigi mali · guvenli yer veya guven icindeki mesken · birinin guvencesi altina girmek · bir seyi birine emanet etmek ve onu guvenilir saymak · zayiflamasindan veya surcmesinden korkulmayan saglam deve · kullarini veya dostlarini zulümden ve azaptan guvende kilan
  الأمن ضد الخوف (ayn;sihah)؛ أصل الأمن طمأنينة النفس وزوال الخوف (mufradat)؛ الأمانة ضد الخيانة ومعناها سكون القلب (maqayis)؛ الأمان إعطاء الأمنة (maqayis;ayn)؛ أمن فلان يأمن أمنا وأمانا وأمنة فهو آمن (tahdhib)؛ استأمن إليه دخل في أمانه (sihah)؛ مأمنه منزله الذي فيه أمنه (mufradat)؛ الأمون الناقة الأمينة الوثيقة أو التي يؤمن فتورها وعثورها (ayn;sihah;mufradat)
- **B002** dogru sayip kabul etme — ozellikle haber veya hakikati dogru kabul etme · herkese guvenen ve duydugunu dogru sayan kisi · kuluna vaat ettigi odulu dogrulayan · bizi dogru sayan veya bize inanan · bildirilen dine girme ve onu kabul etme adi · hakka kalp, dil ve davranisla baglanarak dogru kabul etme · iyi amel anlaminda namaz veya ibadet · guven vermeyen batil seylere guven duymak diye yerilen tutum
  الإيمان التصديق (ayn;sihah)؛ وما أنت بمؤمن لنا أي مصدق لنا (maqayis;ayn;mufradat)؛ إذعان النفس للحق على سبيل التصديق (mufradat)؛ المؤمن في صفات الله يصدق ما وعد عبده (maqayis)
- **B003** duada kabul istegi sozu — duada 'kabul et' veya 'oyle olsun' anlamina gelen soz · ilahi ad oldugu aktarilan dua sozu · duada kabul istegi bildiren sozu soyleme
  قولنا في الدعاء آمين وتفسيره اللهم افعل (maqayis)؛ التأمين من قولك آمين (ayn)؛ آمين في الدعاء يمد ويقصر ومعناه كذلك فليكن (sihah)؛ آمين يقال بالمد والقصر وهو اسم للفعل ومعناه استجب وأمن فلان إذا قال آمين (mufradat)

## ق د م (root_001207): 49:1 تُقَدِّمُوا۟

- **B001** ayak — ayak
  القدم ما يطأ عليه الإنسان (ayn;tahdhib)؛ القدم واحد الأقدام (sihah)؛ القدم قدم الرجل وجمعه أقدام (mufradat)؛ قدم الإنسان معروفة ولعلها سميت بذلك لأنها آلة للتقدم والسبق (maqayis)
- **B002** önceden oluşmuş etki veya paye — öncül etki, iyilik veya saygın konum · önceden kazanılmış iyi paye · önceden işlenmiş kötülük · önder ve saygın kişi · hükümdar veya önder
  القدمة والقدم أيضا السابقة في الأمر وللكافرين قدم شر (ayn)؛ القدم أيضا السابقة في الأمر ولفلان قدم صدق أي أثرة حسنة (sihah)؛ قدم الصدق المنزلة الرفيعة والقدم السابقة وكل ما قدمت من خير والعمل الصالح واليد والمعروف والصنيعة والشرف القديم (tahdhib)؛ متقدم على فلان أي أشرف منه (mufradat)؛ لفلان قدم صدق أي شيء متقدم من أثر حسن والملك هو المقدم (maqayis)؛ رجل قدموس سيد وهو ذلك المعنى (maqayis-variant)
- **B003** eskilik ve önceden var olma — eskilik; sonradan oluşmama · eski; geçmiş zamana dayanan · eskiden; geçmiş zamanda · eski olan
  القدم مصدر القديم من كل شيء (ayn)؛ قدم الشيء فهو قديم والقدم خلاف الحدوث وقدما كان كذا (sihah)؛ القدم العتق مصدر القديم وقد قدم يقدم (tahdhib)؛ حديث وقديم والقدم وجود فيما مضى (mufradat)؛ القدم خلاف الحدوث وشيء قديم إذا كان زمانه سالفا (maqayis)؛ القدموس القديم وأصله من القدم (maqayis-variant)
- **B004** öne geçme, önde ilerleme veya erken davranma — öne geçmek; önde olmak · öne geçme ve ilerleme · ön taraf; önde · önüne geçmek; vaktinden önce davranmak · duraksamadan ileri gitmek
  قدم فلان قومه أي يكون أمامهم والقدم المضي أمام ويمضي قدما أي لا ينثني وقدام خلاف وراء والقدم ضد الأخر (ayn)؛ قدم أي تقدم وقدم بين يديه أي تقدم ومضى قدما لم يعرج ولم ينثن واستقدم وتقدم بمعنى ومقدم نقيض مؤخر (sihah)؛ القدم المضي وهو الإقدام وقدام خلاف وراء والقدم ضد أخر وقدم فلان فلانا إذا تقدمه ولا تقدموا معناه لا تتقدموا قبل الوقت (tahdhib)؛ به اعتبر التقدم والتأخر (mufradat)؛ أصل صحيح يدل على سبق وأصله مضى فلان قدما لم يعرج ولم ينثن ومقدمة الجيش أوله (maqayis)

  البصيرة القطعة من الدم إذا وقعت بالأرض استدارت (maqayis;jamhara)؛ بصائر الدماء طرائقها على الجسد (ayn)؛ البصيرة من الدم ما كان على الأرض وشيء من الدم يستدل به على الرمية (sihah)؛ بصيرة من دم والجدية منها على الأرض والبصيرة الدية ومقدار الدرهم من الدم (tahdhib)؛ البصيرة قطعة من الدم تلمع (mufradat)
- **B005** koruyucu savaş gereci — kalkan, zırh veya giyilen savaş koruması
  البصيرة الترس فيما يقال (maqayis)؛ البصيرة الدرع وما لبس من السلاح فهو بصائر السلاح (ayn;tahdhib)؛ البصيرة في هذا البيت الترس أو الدرع (sihah)؛ الترس اللامع (mufradat)
- **B006** kalın kenar ve ek yeri — bir şeyin yanı, kenarı veya kalın dış yüzü · iki deri parçasını birleştirip dikme · çadır, elbise veya kapta iki parça arası · iki parça arasında yamanmış ek
  بصر الشيء غلظه والبصر هو أن يضم أديم إلى أديم يخاطان والبصيرة ما بين شقتي البيت (maqayis)؛ البصر غلظ الشيء نحو بصر الجبل والسماء والحائط (ayn)؛ بصر كل شيء جلده الظاهر وثوب ذو بصر إذا كان غليظا وثيجا (jamhara)؛ البصر أن يضم أديم إلى أديم والبصر بالضم الجانب والحرف من كل شيء وغلظها (sihah)؛ الباصر الملفق بين شقتين والبصيرة الشقة التي تكون على الخباء والبصر أن يضم أديم إلى أديم (tahdhib)؛ البصر الناحية والبصيرة ما بين شقتي الثوب والمزادة وبصرت الثوب والأديم إذا خطت ذلك الموضع (mufradat)
- **B007** yumuşak parlak taş — yumuşak veya parlak taş; böyle taşlı yer · sonundaki ek düşmüş biçimiyle yumuşak taş · adı bu taşlı yer olan yere gitmek
  البصرة الحجارة الرخوة وبصر بكسر الباء من الأصل الثاني (maqayis)؛ البصرة أرض حجارتها جص والبصرة الحجارة التي فيها بعض اللين (ayn)؛ البصرة حجارة رخوة وبه سميت البصرة (jamhara)؛ البصرة حجارة رخوة إلى البياض وبصر بالكسر (sihah)؛ البصر والبصرة الحجارة البراقة وأرض كأنها جبل من جص وبصر الأرض غلظها وبصر فلان تبصيرا إذا أتى البصرة (tahdhib)؛ البصرة حجارة رخوة تلمع ويقال له بصر (mufradat)

# s049 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. Guarded Thresholds and Interior Admission
- Semantic invariant: A protected interior is approached across a boundary whose crossing must be authorized, patient, and inwardly completed.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:4-5 anchors the chamber, the call from behind it, patient waiting, and emergence, while 49:14 distinguishes verbal surrender from faith entering the heart.
- Surprising reach: Architectural enclosure becomes a model for reason, piety, pardon, and the difference between an exterior declaration and an admitted interior state.

#### Subchannel A. Waiting Outside the Chamber
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Outsiders call across an enclosure; restraint preserves the boundary until the one inside emerges.
- Active motifs: enclosed chamber `quranic:root_000296:B004/m01`; preventing enclosure `quranic:root_000296:B001/m01`; the other side of a barrier `quranic:root_001642:B006/m01`; raised call `quranic:root_001486:B002/m01`; self-restraint `quranic:root_000840:B001/m01`; emergence from an interior `quranic:root_000400:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: 49:4 has the chamber, calling, and "behind"; 49:5 has restraint and emergence.
- Synthesis: The scene is not merely loud speech near a dwelling. The callers remain on the wrong side of a real boundary, and patience is the operation that keeps them there until emergence is granted. Enclosure, distance, call, restraint, and exit therefore form one threshold scene in which correct relation depends on refusing to force access.

#### Subchannel B. From Exterior Surrender to Interior Faith
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A declared handover remains external until belief crosses into the protected heart.
- Active motifs: spoken utterance `quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`; unspoken inner saying `quranic:root_001272:B012/m01`; belief or opinion held as a saying `quranic:root_001272:B013/m01`; corrective reversal with "but" `quranic:root_001373:B002/m01`; entry `quranic:root_000464:B001/m01`; the interior or confidential inside `quranic:root_000464:B003/m01`; handover and surrender `quranic:root_000737:B012/m01`; belief `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; settled safety of the heart `quranic:root_000054:B001/m01`; heart as the seat of understanding `quranic:root_001248:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 15, 16, and 17. Surface anchoring is unavailable for `quranic:root_001373:B002/m01` (`ل ك ن`).
- Synthesis: Speech can carry an inward opinion into public utterance without accomplishing inward admission. The corrective turn interrupts the claim, and the heart remains an interior with its own threshold. Faith must enter and settle there, so declaration reaches the perimeter while conviction may still be outside.

#### Subchannel C. Covering as Protection and Pardon
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A vulnerable interior is preserved by shielding, avoidance, and a cover that can also conceal fault.
- Active motifs: shielding from harm `quranic:root_001677:B001/m01`; placing oneself within protection `quranic:root_001677:B002/m01`; protective covering `quranic:root_001096:B001/m01`; covering a fault in pardon `quranic:root_001096:B002/m01`; atonement that covers a wrong `quranic:root_001307:B009/m01`; moving aside from danger `quranic:root_000262:B003/m01`; reason as a restraining barrier `quranic:root_000296:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: protection occurs at 49:1, 3, 10, 12, and 13; pardon at 49:3, 5, and 14; covering at 49:7; avoidance at 49:12; the chamber root at 49:4.
- Synthesis: Protection is assembled from several compatible operations: a barrier restrains approach, the self moves aside from exposure, and a cover prevents injury or covers an already committed fault. Pardon and atonement extend that cover from prevention to repair. The chamber boundary is thus reframed as an ethical technology protecting both what is within and the person who might otherwise trespass.

### 2. Vertical Calibration of Voice and Standing
- Semantic invariant: Social relation is made audible and spatial through controlled height: voices rise or lower, and persons are elevated by genuine standing rather than self-assertion.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:2-3 calibrates voice above and below the prophetic voice, while 49:8, 11, and 13 pressure rival claims to distinction and honor.
- Surprising reach: Acoustic altitude becomes a diagnostic of social rank, yet the surah reverses the expected ladder by locating durable elevation in restraint and protected character.

#### Subchannel A. Lowering the Acoustic Level
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A voice is raised into audibility, compared with a higher reference line, and deliberately reduced.
- Active motifs: audible voice `quranic:root_000890:B001/m01`; physical raising `quranic:root_000582:B001/m01`; loudness `quranic:root_000582:B010/m01`; being above `quranic:root_001188:B001/m01`; public loud utterance `quranic:root_000269:B001/m01`; voiced consonant formed by controlled airflow `quranic:root_000269:B011/m01`; lowering and restraining `quranic:root_001093:B001/m01`; ear as the receiving instrument `quranic:root_000741:B002/m01`; hearing sound `quranic:root_000741:B001/m01`; prophet as divine messenger `quranic:root_001464:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 3, and 6.
- Synthesis: The relevant action is measurable adjustment, not silence. Breath and articulation produce a voiced sound, the ear receives it, and the speaker places it on a vertical scale beneath the prophetic reference voice. Restraint therefore changes both acoustic force and the relation between speaker and messenger.

#### Subchannel B. Received Standing
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Standing is elevated by tested quality, prior good, and conferred distinction rather than by the claimant's own lift.
- Active motifs: elevation in rank `quranic:root_000582:B002/m01`; height or loftiness `quranic:root_000745:B001/m01`; superior rank `quranic:root_001188:B002/m01`; granted distinction `quranic:root_001163:B002/m01`; inherent honor `quranic:root_001294:B001/m01`; greatness `quranic:root_001029:B001/m01`; public honor and inviolability `quranic:root_001029:B010/m01`; prior standing in good `quranic:root_001207:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: elevation and "above" occur at 49:2-3; greatness and reward at 49:3; distinction at 49:8; the name/height root at 49:11, 16, and 18; honor at 49:13; precedence at 49:1.
- Synthesis: Rank language extends the voice scale into a social hierarchy, but its stable forms are received or recognized: tested quality, prior good, granted distinction, and protected honor. The scale is real, yet persons do not establish their own position merely by speaking from above.

#### Subchannel C. Rival Self-Elevation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A claimant tries to rise by outnumbering, excessive talk, competitive generosity, and self-magnification.
- Active motifs: claiming superiority `quranic:root_001163:B004/m01`; outnumbering and boasting `quranic:root_001286:B002/m01`; excessive talk or claims `quranic:root_001286:B003/m01`; competing to defeat another in generosity `quranic:root_001294:B006/m01`; rivalry for name or standing `quranic:root_000745:B007/m01`; self-magnification and pride `quranic:root_001029:B006/m01`; competition for a prized object `quranic:root_001533:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:3, 4, 7, 8, 11, 12, 13, 15, 16, and 18.
- Synthesis: This is the counter-scene to received standing. Number, speech, gifts, and valued possessions are turned into instruments for climbing over peers. Because each instrument depends on public comparison, the resulting altitude remains performative and unstable.

### 3. Disciplined Knowing
- Semantic invariant: Reliable knowledge requires controlled passage from arrival or concealment to disclosure, examination, and accountable judgment.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:6 stages the arrival and clarification of news, 49:12 governs suspicion and covert inquiry, and 49:13, 16, and 18 contrast human recognition with encompassing knowledge of the hidden.
- Surprising reach: Epistemic discipline is materialized as target practice, unmarked terrain, tactile probing, a dawn raid, and the cleaning of a well until what it holds becomes visible.

#### Subchannel A. News Arrives Before Judgment
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A report travels to its recipients, is made clear, and is checked before an ignorant strike produces regret.
- Active motifs: movement and arrival `quranic:root_001464:B001/m01`; report or news `quranic:root_001464:B002/m01`; coming to a place `quranic:root_000281:B001/m01`; bringing something to another `quranic:root_000281:B004/m01`; disclosure and clarification `quranic:root_000170:B004/m01`; meaning exposed by speech or sign `quranic:root_000170:B005/m01`; lack of knowledge `quranic:root_000271:B001/m01`; correctness in word and act `quranic:root_000889:B002/m01`; hitting a target correctly `quranic:root_000889:B003/m01`; a calamity striking its object `quranic:root_000889:B004/m01`; becoming fixed in a resulting state `quranic:root_000839:B010/m01`; regret after the act `quranic:root_001485:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 6, 9, and 10.
- Synthesis: Information is treated as a moving object that reaches a decision point before action does. Clarification by speech or sign interrupts the sequence between arrival and impact. Correctness must precede the strike; otherwise an intendedly accurate act becomes the calamity that fixes the actors in regret.

#### Subchannel B. Suspicion Becomes Invasive Search
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Unsettled supposition hardens into accusation, then crosses another person's boundary through covert probing and speech in absence.
- Active motifs: uncertain supposition `quranic:root_000969:B003/m01`; accusation built on conjecture `quranic:root_000969:B004/m01`; bad suspicion `quranic:root_000969:B005/m01`; gentle tactile probing `quranic:root_000246:B001/m01`; covert intelligence gathering `quranic:root_000246:B002/m01`; senses used as probing instruments `quranic:root_000246:B003/m01`; probing by sight `quranic:root_000246:B004/m01`; hearing or seeing nothing yet acting on supposition `quranic:root_000741:B013/m01`; hidden or absent state `quranic:root_001117:B001/m01`; mentioning an absent person `quranic:root_001117:B004/m01`; concealment by indirect disclosure `quranic:root_001642:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 4, 12, and 18.
- Synthesis: The scene advances from an unstable interior judgment to bodily and informational trespass. Touch, eye, ear, and covert intelligence become alternate instruments of the same invasive search, even when perception has supplied nothing. Speech about the absent person is the terminal public release of what the search presumed or extracted.

#### Subchannel C. Recognition Under Encompassing Knowledge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Human observers recognize by signs and inward understanding, while divine knowledge includes what remains absent from sight.
- Active motifs: knowing and distinguishing `quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`; report joined to inward knowledge `quranic:root_000387:B001/m01`; eyesight `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`; insight of the heart `quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`; the hidden and absent `quranic:root_001117:B001/m01`; all parts encompassed as a whole `quranic:root_001315:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 7, 8, 12, 13, 16, and 18.
- Synthesis: Seeing, insight, and informed report give human recognition several channels, but each remains sign-dependent. Knowledge of the absent encompasses the whole rather than only the disclosed part. Persons who know one another through marks and reports should therefore not act as though they possess the complete access named at the surah's close.

#### Subchannel D. Clearing the Well of Evidence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An obscured source is stripped, cleaned, and opened until its contents can be brought into view.
- Active motifs: unlined well as receptacle `quranic:root_001248:B007/m01`; cleaning a well until its water appears `quranic:root_000269:B008/m01`; stripping or clearing a well `quranic:root_001403:B005/m01`; extracting what was hidden `quranic:root_000400:B002/m01`; making a matter clear `quranic:root_000170:B004/m01`; water basin or collection point `quranic:root_000281:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, and 14.
- Synthesis: These latent senses assemble a concrete model of verification. Evidence is not manufactured; an obstructed receptacle is cleared so that what was already present can appear and be drawn out. Making the heart-root itself name a well connects epistemic clearing to interior testing without turning verification into suspicion's invasive probing.

#### Subchannel E. Unmarked Ground and Agitated Motion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler without landmarks loses orientation, becomes agitated, and moves before a route has been established.
- Active motifs: impulsive disturbance opposed to composure `quranic:root_000271:B002/m01`; unknown terrain without mark or prior use `quranic:root_000271:B003/m01`; agitation after stillness `quranic:root_000271:B005/m01`; road marker and appointed sign `quranic:root_000051:B005/m01`; traversing land without knowledge `quranic:root_000269:B009/m01`; landmark for orientation `quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ignorance occurs at 49:6; command/marker at 49:7 and 9; the public-voice root at 49:2; knowing/marking at 49:1, 7, 8, 13, 16, and 18.
- Synthesis: Ignorance is not inert emptiness here. It is an unmarked setting that provokes restless motion: the traveler has neither prior knowledge nor a sign by which to orient. The scene explains why clarification must precede action, because agitation on unknown ground amplifies error rather than resolving it.

#### Subchannel F. The Report Becomes a Dawn Raid
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An arriving report triggers a surprise approach at daybreak, turning unverified information into an attack on an unprepared group.
- Active motifs: dawn and first light `quranic:root_000839:B001/m01`; arrival in the morning `quranic:root_000839:B002/m01`; morning raid and distress call `quranic:root_000839:B004/m01`; coming upon people at dawn unawares `quranic:root_000269:B005/m01`; calamity striking its object `quranic:root_000889:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the morning/state root and the striking root occur at 49:6; the public-arrival root occurs at 49:2.
- Synthesis: The latent scene supplies a concrete form for harming people in ignorance. News arrives, armed movement follows before full visibility, and an unsuspecting group receives the strike. Morning is therefore not a neutral time marker: it is the interval in which premature certainty becomes irreversible action.

#### Subchannel G. Warranted Inference Versus an Unreliable Guess
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A sign supports recognition and a bounded expectation; where no reliable sign or outcome exists, inference must stop.
- Active motifs: conviction grounded in an indication `quranic:root_000969:B001/m01`; expected location of a thing `quranic:root_000969:B002/m01`; an outcome or water source that cannot be trusted `quranic:root_000969:B006/m01`; fine perception and awareness `quranic:root_000798:B005/m01`; visible sign or ritual marker `quranic:root_000798:B006/m01`; recognition from a trace `quranic:root_001002:B003/m01`; identifying lost property by its description `quranic:root_001002:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: perception occurs at 49:2; conjecture at 49:12; recognition at 49:13.
- Synthesis: The scene distinguishes inference from suspicion rather than rejecting all expectation. Marks and descriptions can identify what is sought, but an unreliable source cannot sustain confidence about its outcome. This boundary blocks the slide from limited evidence into accusation and invasive search.

### 4. Directed Alignment Under Authority
- Semantic invariant: Right relation consists in taking a governed direction: not moving ahead of the guide, accepting restraint, and returning after deviation.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:1 prohibits advance before authority, 49:7 names guidance and obedience, 49:9 orders return from aggression, 49:15 places exertion on a way, and 49:16 names religion under encompassing knowledge.
- Surprising reach: Guidance is embodied as procession, measured travel, bridle, tether, city, and road geometry, so obedience appears as coordinated motion rather than passive immobility.

#### Subchannel A. Following the Front of the Procession
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A guide occupies the front, a message establishes direction, and followers advance on the marked way without overtaking.
- Active motifs: advancing `quranic:root_001207:B004/m01`; bold advance `quranic:root_001207:B006/m01`; the front `quranic:root_001207:B007/m01`; being before another `quranic:root_001693:B008/m01`; leader at the front `quranic:root_001583:B003/m01`; gentle indication toward the way `quranic:root_001583:B001/m01`; direction and manner of proceeding `quranic:root_001583:B002/m01`; straight guidance `quranic:root_000565:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: advance and "before" occur at 49:1; straight guidance at 49:7; guidance and its direction at 49:17.
- Synthesis: The prohibition on putting oneself forward becomes a procession scene. The front is already occupied, and guidance supplies both direction and a manner of proceeding. Bold advance becomes disorder when it overtakes that position, so valid motion follows rather than manufactures the orientation.

#### Subchannel B. The Messenger Travels at a Measured Pace
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A messenger is dispatched along a clear road, moves with an easy gait, and lets successive groups follow without haste.
- Active motifs: dispatch and release `quranic:root_000563:B001/m01`; message-bearing messenger `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`; easy gait and supple movement `quranic:root_000563:B003/m01`; deliberation and measured pace `quranic:root_000563:B004/m01`; successive companies `quranic:root_000563:B005/m01`; road `quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`; road traveler `quranic:root_000672:B002/m01`; clear easy road `quranic:root_001464:B007/m01`; calm bearing without panicked haste `quranic:root_001583:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the messenger root occurs at 49:1, 3, 7, 14, and 15; road/traveler at 49:15; news/prophetic-road root at 49:2 and 6; guidance at 49:17.
- Synthesis: This is a travel operation distinct from rank in the procession. Dispatch initiates motion, the road provides a clear course, and deliberation governs speed. Successive groups can follow because the messenger's progress is steady rather than erratic or panicked.

#### Subchannel C. Bridle, Tether, and Handover
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Forceful independent motion is brought into alignment through restraint, attachment, and voluntary handover.
- Active motifs: intellect that restrains ignorance `quranic:root_001036:B001/m01`; camel tether `quranic:root_001036:B002/m01`; held tongue `quranic:root_001036:B003/m01`; handover and surrender `quranic:root_000737:B012/m01`; submission under another's hand `quranic:root_001693:B006/m01`; bridle that restrains and corrects `quranic:root_000348:B006/m01`; tether or binding tie `quranic:root_000020:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: reason/tether occurs at 49:4; the hand root at 49:1; wisdom and restraint at 49:8; the brother/tie root at 49:10 and 12; surrender at 49:14 and 17.
- Synthesis: These senses materialize governed obedience without collapsing it into compulsion. Intellect, tongue, animal motion, and social relation are each held by an appropriate restraint. Bridle and tether check direction, while handover yields control, explaining how external surrender can be real alignment while remaining distinct from inward faith.

#### Subchannel D. Who Obeys Whom
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Followers try to make the messenger conform to their preference, but the proper direction of obedience runs from the community toward the governing command.
- Active motifs: obedience to an order `quranic:root_000956:B001/m01`; mutual conformity `quranic:root_000956:B002/m01`; the self making an act easy `quranic:root_000956:B006/m01`; weak dependent opinion that follows everyone `quranic:root_000051:B008/m01`; imposing intolerable difficulty `quranic:root_001051:B003/m01`; message-bearing messenger `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: obedience occurs at 49:7 and 14; affair and dependent opinion at 49:7 and 9; hardship at 49:7; the messenger root at 49:1, 3, 7, 14, and 15.
- Synthesis: The scene is a role reversal test. If the messenger becomes the compliant party in every affair, collective preference governs and hardship follows. Right obedience instead trains the self to move readily under a command whose direction it does not invent.

#### Subchannel E. Command Within a Governed Polity
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A public affair is held by authority, issued as command, delegated for judgment, and maintained as an ordered social place.
- Active motifs: public affair or circumstance `quranic:root_000051:B001/m01`; binding command `quranic:root_000051:B002/m01`; authority and office `quranic:root_000051:B003/m01`; obedience as religion `quranic:root_000504:B001/m01`; city organized by obedience to authority `quranic:root_000504:B006/m01`; delegated judgment `quranic:root_000348:B005/m01`; governing care and maintenance `quranic:root_001273:B004/m01`; effective sovereign hand `quranic:root_001693:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: affair, command, and authority occur at 49:7 and 9; wisdom/judgment at 49:8; people and governing care at 49:6 and 11; religion at 49:16; the hand root at 49:1.
- Synthesis: Authority is not only a superior voice. It is an institutional scene with an affair to manage, command to issue, judgment to delegate, and community to maintain. The city image gives obedience a social architecture in which order depends on care as well as enforceable direction.

#### Subchannel F. Deviation, Overreach, and Return
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A party leaves the line, moves beyond its limit, and must be turned back to the governing course.
- Active motifs: resistance and deviation `quranic:root_001052:B001/m01`; moving aside `quranic:root_001052:B002/m01`; uncontrolled flow running sideways `quranic:root_001052:B003/m01`; departure from the straight line `quranic:root_000991:B006/m01`; injustice as deviation `quranic:root_001224:B002/m01`; overstepping in aggression `quranic:root_000138:B003/m01`; leaving group or obedience `quranic:root_000400:B006/m01`; ripe fruit breaking through its peel `quranic:root_001156:B002/m01`; disobedience `quranic:root_001022:B001/m01`; return `quranic:root_001191:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: resistance occurs at 49:3 and 13; departure at 49:5; breach at 49:6, 7, and 11; disobedience at 49:7 and 11; deviation, aggression, and return at 49:9.
- Synthesis: Aggression is represented as path error and boundary breach before it is treated as a fixed identity. Motion runs sideways, fruit breaks its containing peel, and a faction exits obedience. Because the failure is directional, return remains an available restorative operation.

### 5. Repairing the Social Body
- Semantic invariant: Fractured relations are restored by stopping violence, rejoining separated parts, and setting the whole to a just line and measure.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:9-10 presents fighting factions, return, equitable settlement, brotherhood, and reconciliation.
- Surprising reach: Social repair takes material form as patching a vessel, draining a wound, setting a bone, hearing a grievance, balancing a load, and weaving kinship tissue.

#### Subchannel A. Returning Factions to Peace
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Two parties fight; aggression is restrained, the transgressing party returns, and the divided parties are reconciled.
- Active motifs: faction or group `quranic:root_000957:B004/m01`; mutual fighting `quranic:root_001200:B011/m01`; killing `quranic:root_001200:B001/m01`; exposing another to death `quranic:root_001200:B005/m01`; confrontation and combat `quranic:root_001273:B014/m01`; aggressive overreach `quranic:root_000138:B003/m01`; return `quranic:root_001191:B001/m01`; a faction returning to its leader `quranic:root_001191:B004/m01`; reconciliation `quranic:root_000876:B002/m01`; peace `quranic:root_000737:B004/m01`; restored bond between parties `quranic:root_000170:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 6, 9, 10, 11, 14, and 17.
- Synthesis: The sequence begins with genuine exposure to death and has an endpoint beyond cessation of combat. A separated faction returns, peace replaces reciprocal force, and reconciliation restores a relation between the parties. Return is the hinge that converts coercive restraint into renewed social connection.

#### Subchannel B. Patching the Cracked Vessel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Split material is brought edge-to-edge, stitched or soldered, and made fit to hold its contents again.
- Active motifs: mending a crack `quranic:root_000797:B002/m01`; patched water skin whose seams can leak `quranic:root_000797:B007/m01`; soldering or joining a fissure `quranic:root_001348:B005/m01`; stitching two hides or cloth edges `quranic:root_000121:B006/m01`; repair after corruption `quranic:root_000876:B001/m01`; restored fit and suitability `quranic:root_000876:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: repair occurs at 49:9-10; flesh/solder at 49:12; branching and patched-vessel imagery at 49:13; sight/stitching at 49:18.
- Synthesis: This repair operation concerns continuity and function, not bodily recovery. Edges must meet, the joining material must hold, and the vessel must again suit its purpose without leaking. Reconciliation is thereby pictured as restoration of a shared container rather than mere proximity of its pieces.

#### Subchannel C. Treating Wound, Relapse, and Fracture
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A wound is drained and filled, a broken bone is set, and the repair is guarded against renewed corruption.
- Active motifs: a wound filling and healing `quranic:root_001348:B008/m01`; setting a broken bone `quranic:root_000015:B002/m01`; refracturing a bone `quranic:root_001051:B004/m01`; relapse of a wound `quranic:root_001096:B004/m01`; wound swelling toward corruption `quranic:root_000138:B004/m01`; pus collected in a wound `quranic:root_000281:B006/m01`; abscess emerging from the body `quranic:root_000400:B004/m01`; wound reopening and leaving a trace `quranic:root_000289:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: wound invalidation occurs at 49:2; setting at 49:3; abscess/emergence at 49:5; pus/arrival at 49:6; refracture at 49:7; wound corruption/aggression at 49:9; flesh at 49:12; relapse/covering at 49:3, 5, and 14.
- Synthesis: The sequence separates initial closure from durable recovery. Pus and swelling must leave the interior, the fracture must be set, and relapse can still undo apparent healing. This materially pressures both reconciliation and the warning that a completed work can be nullified after it seemed secure.

#### Subchannel D. Entrusting the Grievance to a Wise Arbiter
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An injured party presents a grievance to a delegated judge whose knowledge produces a firm and equitable decision.
- Active motifs: judicial decision between people `quranic:root_000348:B002/m01`; wisdom that reaches the right judgment `quranic:root_000348:B003/m01`; firm and secured decision `quranic:root_000348:B004/m01`; delegated arbitration `quranic:root_000348:B005/m01`; grievance seeking redress `quranic:root_000967:B003/m01`; justice `quranic:root_000991:B001/m01`; equitable dealing `quranic:root_001224:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: wisdom and judgment occur at 49:8; justice and equity at 49:9; grievance/wrongdoing at 49:11.
- Synthesis: Settlement requires a competent decision-maker, not only willing parties. The grievance supplies the claim, delegated authority supplies jurisdiction, wisdom identifies the right outcome, and firmness prevents the decision from dissolving under renewed pressure.

#### Subchannel E. Setting the Line and Balance
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A crooked or unequal arrangement is restrained, measured, counterweighted, and straightened.
- Active motifs: equivalence `quranic:root_000991:B002/m01`; counterbalanced load `quranic:root_000991:B004/m01`; straightening `quranic:root_000991:B005/m01`; allotted share `quranic:root_001224:B003/m01`; balance `quranic:root_001224:B004/m01`; crooked or stiff legs `quranic:root_001224:B005/m01`; restraint for correction `quranic:root_000348:B001/m01`; straightness `quranic:root_001273:B008/m01`; equal weight `quranic:root_001273:B015/m01`; wrong as placing a thing outside its proper time or place `quranic:root_000967:B004/m01`; withholding another's right `quranic:root_000967:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:6, 8, 9, and 11.
- Synthesis: Justice is the mechanical work of returning misplaced weight and withheld shares to proportion. An unequal load, a crooked support, and diverging parties are measured against a line. Restraint is justified by that reparative outcome: it must restore place and right rather than reproduce domination.

#### Subchannel F. Reweaving Kinship
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Separated social parts are rebound as a fabric or living tissue whose continuity constitutes kinship.
- Active motifs: brotherhood `quranic:root_000020:B001/m01`; binding tie `quranic:root_000020:B002/m01`; kinship relation `quranic:root_000552:B002/m01`; truthful friendship `quranic:root_000852:B005/m01`; woven weft `quranic:root_001348:B006/m01`; flesh as kinship tissue `quranic:root_001348:B007/m01`; tightly twisted interlocking cord `quranic:root_001348:B012/m01`; bond between parties `quranic:root_000170:B003/m01`; people as branches of a whole `quranic:root_000797:B003/m01`; fitted facing parts `quranic:root_001198:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 5, 6, 9, 10, 12, 13, 14, 15, and 17.
- Synthesis: Brotherhood is a structural relation rather than a decorative title. Tie, weft, tissue, twisted cord, and fitted part all describe plurality held in continuity, while truthful friendship names its social quality. Reconciliation consequently repairs the material that makes the parties relatives in the first place.

### 6. Harmful Intake and Hidden Injury
- Semantic invariant: What enters or leaves the mouth can violate a protected relation and spread corruption through a social or bodily organism.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:12 converts speech about the absent into eating the dead flesh of a brother, while related lexical imagery supplies the bodily consequences of corrupt intake.
- Surprising reach: Backbiting is pressured by veterinary pathology, burial, shared bodily tissue, and a concealed spark that turns circulating talk into social fire.

#### Subchannel A. Consuming the Absent Brother
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Speech about an absent relative is enacted as stripping and eating flesh from a dead body.
- Active motifs: mentioning an absent person `quranic:root_001117:B004/m01`; burial that removes a body from sight `quranic:root_001117:B009/m01`; eating `quranic:root_000043:B001/m01`; flesh or meat `quranic:root_001348:B001/m01`; stripping flesh from bone `quranic:root_001348:B009/m01`; kinship tissue `quranic:root_001348:B007/m01`; death `quranic:root_001454:B001/m01`; carrion `quranic:root_001454:B007/m01`; brotherhood `quranic:root_000020:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:10, 12, and 18.
- Synthesis: The comparison forms a complete bodily scene. Absence and burial remove the victim from answer and sight, death removes resistance, and kinship makes the consumed flesh one's own social tissue. The stripping sense intensifies the operation: backbiting dismantles the body that carries the bond.

#### Subchannel B. Toxic Feeding Swells Within
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Appetite takes in harmful matter; concealed corruption develops internally as swelling near death.
- Active motifs: livestock swelling from toxic fodder `quranic:root_000289:B001/m01`; prepared food or prey `quranic:root_000043:B007/m01`; corrosion and bodily decay `quranic:root_000043:B006/m01`; appetite for meat `quranic:root_001348:B002/m01`; appetite pressed toward food `quranic:root_000268:B006/m01`; disease that eats the innards `quranic:root_001642:B001/m01`; internal swelling or fullness `quranic:root_001470:B006/m01`; hidden inward corruption `quranic:root_000464:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the swelling/invalidating root occurs at 49:2; the inward-disease root at 49:4; eating and flesh at 49:12; inward entry at 49:14; exertion/appetite at 49:15. Surface anchoring is unavailable for `quranic:root_001470:B006/m01` (`ن ب و`).
- Synthesis: This is a distinct consequence scene rather than another rendering of the corpse meal. Appetite receives harmful matter as food, but a disease eats the eater from within while hidden corruption swells toward collapse. The inversion makes socially appetitive speech look nourishing at intake and predatory after admission.

#### Subchannel C. Circulating Talk Kindles Social Fire
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A concealed spark is struck, speech is released into circulation, and tale-bearing feeds a fire between groups.
- Active motifs: latent fire brought from a flint `quranic:root_001642:B002/m01`; fire consuming its fuel `quranic:root_000043:B005/m01`; tale-bearing that stirs corruption between people `quranic:root_000043:B009/m01`; talk circulating among people `quranic:root_001272:B007/m01`; broadcasting a report `quranic:root_000582:B005/m01`; shame-producing words `quranic:root_001486:B005/m01`; mentioning the absent `quranic:root_001117:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:2, 4, 12, 14, 16, 17, and 18.
- Synthesis: The fire scene describes propagation rather than intake. A hidden spark becomes flame only after release; circulating talk likewise becomes destructive when a broadcaster and tale-bearer supply fuel and reach. Shameful speech can therefore move from a private utterance to a force that consumes relations between groups.

#### Subchannel D. Faulting the Other Injures the Shared Self
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A fault first held within the self becomes a verbal or bodily jab against a brother who belongs to the same social tissue.
- Active motifs: self as the very same entity `quranic:root_001533:B012/m01`; inward intention and thought `quranic:root_001533:B013/m01`; faulting or slanderous speech `quranic:root_001376:B001/m01`; physical or verbal jab `quranic:root_001376:B002/m01`; brotherhood `quranic:root_000020:B001/m01`; flesh as kinship tissue `quranic:root_001348:B007/m01`; self or life `quranic:root_001533:B011/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:10, 11, 12, and 15.
- Synthesis: The plural self in 49:11 is materialized as shared tissue. An inward judgment crosses into a jab, but its target remains part of the same brotherly body. Faulting another is therefore reflexive injury rather than an attack that leaves the speaker's own social self intact.

### 7. The Cultivated and Tested Interior
- Semantic invariant: The inward person is a material core whose condition is disclosed by testing, adorned by attachment, and cultivated toward growth.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:3, 7, and 14 foreground tested hearts, beloved faith, adornment, and faith not yet entering the heart.
- Surprising reach: The heart becomes refined material, adorned interior, protected kernel, and worked field, while covering can either preserve growth or suppress truth.

#### Subchannel A. Testing and Refining the Heart
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Testing exposes the core, refinement removes admixture, and settled protection preserves the resulting interior state.
- Active motifs: testing that reveals the heart `quranic:root_001403:B001/m01`; refining and purifying material `quranic:root_001403:B002/m01`; stripping away obscuring matter `quranic:root_001403:B005/m01`; heart and understanding `quranic:root_001248:B001/m01`; inner core or essence `quranic:root_001248:B002/m01`; black core of the heart `quranic:root_000286:B004/m01`; turning the heart from one face to another `quranic:root_001248:B004/m01`; disease within the heart `quranic:root_001248:B011/m01`; self held within protection `quranic:root_001677:B002/m01`; settled security of the heart `quranic:root_000054:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, and 17.
- Synthesis: The test does not add a public label to the heart; it exposes what occupies the core and whether that interior turns or carries disease. Purification removes obscuring matter, and protective settling preserves the refined condition. Restraint of voice can therefore register an interior that has survived testing rather than merely performed compliance.

#### Subchannel B. Faith Adorns the Interior
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Belief becomes a loved attachment whose beauty is placed within the heart rather than displayed as external rank.
- Active motifs: love and attachment `quranic:root_000286:B002/m01`; beauty free from blemish `quranic:root_000660:B001/m01`; ornament applied to a person or thing `quranic:root_000660:B003/m01`; belief `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; heart and understanding `quranic:root_001248:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 15, and 17.
- Synthesis: Adornment is an inward placement operation. Love fastens belief to the heart, and beauty names the resulting freedom from blemish. The scene distinguishes an interior made beautiful by attachment from a reputation ornamented for public comparison.

#### Subchannel C. Seed Sheltered in Pith and Sheath
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A living kernel is placed under cover, held in plant pith, and protected by a fruit sheath before emergence.
- Active motifs: seed `quranic:root_000286:B001/m01`; palm pith or inner plant core `quranic:root_001248:B003/m01`; generic protective cover `quranic:root_001307:B001/m01`; burying or covering seed `quranic:root_001307:B008/m01`; fruit sheath `quranic:root_001307:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: seed/love occurs at 49:7, 9, and 12; heart/pith at 49:3, 7, and 14; covering at 49:7.
- Synthesis: Concealment is productive when it protects a viable interior. Soil, pith, and sheath do not deny the seed; they preserve it through the interval before manifestation. This makes entry into the heart comparable to germination rather than the mere repetition of a public formula.

#### Subchannel D. Cultivated Ground Bears Grain
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A cultivator works soft, watered earth until vegetation forms a seed-bearing crop.
- Active motifs: soft fertile ground `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`; easy fertile earth `quranic:root_000058:B004/m01`; fertile rain and soil `quranic:root_001294:B002/m01`; low soft land that gathers water `quranic:root_000387:B002/m01`; cultivation for a known share of yield `quranic:root_000387:B003/m01`; tender plant growth `quranic:root_000387:B005/m01`; earth adorned by vegetation `quranic:root_000660:B002/m01`; crop forming seed `quranic:root_001348:B011/m01`; ear of grain `quranic:root_000672:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: land occurs at 49:16 and 18; female/fertility, informed knowledge, and honor at 49:13; adornment at 49:7; flesh/crop at 49:12; road/grain at 49:15.
- Synthesis: This is field cultivation rather than kernel protection. Soil, retained water, skilled labor, and a known share of yield supply setting, agent, process, and outcome. The scene pressures claims of inward faith by asking whether the interior has been worked into visible grain.

#### Subchannel E. Covering Can Preserve or Suppress
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The same covering operation either shelters life or hides truth and received favor from acknowledgment.
- Active motifs: generic covering `quranic:root_001307:B001/m01`; covering seed `quranic:root_001307:B008/m01`; denial that covers truth `quranic:root_001307:B003/m01`; ingratitude that covers favor `quranic:root_001307:B004/m01`; belief `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; unowed gift or favor `quranic:root_001163:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 15, and 17.
- Synthesis: Covering is evaluated by what it does to its interior. Soil protects seed until life appears, while denial and ingratitude conceal truth or gift so that neither can be acknowledged. The contrast sharpens the pairing of beloved faith and hated disbelief without reducing the root to a single moral label.

### 8. Made Plurality and Social Marking
- Semantic invariant: Human plurality is formed from one measured origin and becomes socially legible through faces, signs, group structure, and names.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:11 governs mocking and injurious naming, while 49:13 joins creation, sex, peoples, tribes, recognition, and honor.
- Surprising reach: The same social world can be born through one womb, assembled as fitted branches, made familiar through encounter, or damaged when mockery and hostile labels manufacture distance and reputation.

#### Subchannel A. One Origin Branches into Peoples
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A measured act of making produces male and female, then branches the resulting whole into facing and fitted groups.
- Active motifs: measuring before formation `quranic:root_000434:B001/m01`; creating `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`; making or appointing `quranic:root_000248:B001/m01`; transforming into a social state `quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`; male `quranic:root_000516:B001/m01`; female `quranic:root_000058:B001/m01`; people as a social group `quranic:root_001273:B001/m01`; branching and separation `quranic:root_000797:B001/m01`; peoples as major branches `quranic:root_000797:B003/m01`; branch or offshoot `quranic:root_000797:B005/m01`; tribes facing one another `quranic:root_001198:B009/m01`; fitted facing parts `quranic:root_001198:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: creation, sex, peoples, branches, and tribes occur at 49:13; the group root also occurs at 49:6 and 11.
- Synthesis: Plurality is not presented as disconnected manufacture. A measured origin is transformed into differentiated groups whose branches still face and fit one another. Peoples and tribes are legible subdivisions of one made whole, which makes superiority by branch structurally incoherent.

#### Subchannel B. Recognition by Face and Sign
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Persons meet face-to-face and identify one another through visible marks interpreted by informed recognition.
- Active motifs: fine perception and awareness `quranic:root_000798:B005/m01`; visible sign or marker `quranic:root_000798:B006/m01`; recognition by a sign or trace `quranic:root_001002:B003/m01`; identifying a lost object by its description `quranic:root_001002:B008/m01`; a representative who knows a group `quranic:root_001002:B006/m01`; visible identifying features `quranic:root_001002:B013/m01`; face-to-face encounter `quranic:root_001198:B001/m01`; distinguishing mark or landmark `quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`; report joined to inward knowledge `quranic:root_000387:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 7, 8, 13, 16, and 18.
- Synthesis: Recognition requires presentation, a discriminating sign, and an interpreter. The lost-object scene shows the mechanism cleanly: description and trace guide recognition without becoming the object itself. Faces and group marks likewise orient mutual knowledge without licensing reduction of a person to one visible feature.

#### Subchannel C. Names That Wound Identity
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person's social name is replaced by an added epithet, faulting remark, or verbal jab that fixes dishonor to identity.
- Active motifs: name `quranic:root_000745:B005/m01`; added epithet `quranic:root_001367:B001/m01`; insulting nickname `quranic:root_001467:B001/m01`; word-form of blame `quranic:root_000079:B004/m01`; branding another an unbeliever `quranic:root_001307:B006/m01`; faulting or slanderous speech `quranic:root_001376:B001/m01`; physical or verbal jab `quranic:root_001376:B002/m01`; speech about the absent `quranic:root_001117:B004/m01`; cherished or honored person `quranic:root_001294:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:7, 11, 12, 13, 16, and 18.
- Synthesis: The hostile label is an action upon social identity, not merely an inaccurate descriptor. Blame, epithet, and religious branding detach a fault from a moment and attach it to the person's standing. Juxtaposed with the cherished person and mutual recognition, the scene distinguishes knowing someone from verbally manufacturing who they are.

#### Subchannel D. From Estrangement to Familiar Company
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A previously unknown presence is detected by sight or hearing, turns its familiar side toward another, and becomes companion rather than source of estrangement.
- Active motifs: human presence opposed to wild isolation `quranic:root_000059:B001/m01`; detecting a presence by sight, sound, or felt awareness `quranic:root_000059:B002/m01`; familiarity that removes estrangement `quranic:root_000059:B003/m01`; the inward-facing familiar side `quranic:root_000059:B004/m01`; human image visible in the pupil `quranic:root_000059:B005/m01`; intimate companion and chosen associate `quranic:root_000059:B006/m01`; recognition by a trace `quranic:root_001002:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: recognition occurs at 49:13. Surface anchoring is unavailable for all `quranic:root_000059` motifs (`ء ن س`).
- Synthesis: Mutual recognition has a social outcome beyond identification. Perception discovers another presence, face-to-face orientation replaces the wild or alien side with a familiar one, and repeated company removes estrangement. The image of another human reflected in the pupil makes recognition literally relational.

#### Subchannel E. Outward Form and Inward Character
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A completed visible form is distinguished from the inward disposition by which a person actually lives among others.
- Active motifs: completed and proportioned visible form `quranic:root_000434:B003/m01`; inward disposition and character `quranic:root_000434:B004/m01`; readiness or fitness for a quality `quranic:root_000434:B005/m01`; eyesight `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`; insight of the heart `quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`; inherent honor `quranic:root_001294:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: form, character, and honor are rooted at 49:13; eyesight and insight occur at 49:18.
- Synthesis: Creation supplies both visible shape and an inward manner of being. Eyes can inspect the finished image, but insight is required for character and readiness. The distinction usefully pressures rank by appearance: honor belongs to formed conduct, not merely an inspectable exterior.

#### Subchannel F. Mockery as Social Subjugation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A mocker assumes superior standing, reduces another to an instrument or unpaid subordinate, and judges worth without knowing who is better.
- Active motifs: subjugating a thing for one's use `quranic:root_000685:B001/m01`; forced labor without wage `quranic:root_000685:B002/m01`; ridicule and mockery `quranic:root_000685:B003/m01`; beneficial good `quranic:root_000452:B001/m01`; selected excellence `quranic:root_000452:B002/m01`; choosing what is better `quranic:root_000452:B003/m01`; claiming superiority `quranic:root_001163:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: benefit and patient waiting occur at 49:5; distinction at 49:8; mockery and the possibility of the other's being better occur at 49:11.
- Synthesis: Mockery is a hierarchy-making act. Its same-root senses show the inferior treated as usable or exploitable, while the comparative-good senses expose the mocker's lack of grounds for selection. The scene reframes ridicule as an unauthorized assignment of rank and role.

#### Subchannel G. Utterance Becomes Reputation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Mention is made audible, broadcast, and circulated until it settles as a public name attached to a person.
- Active motifs: mentioning a person by tongue `quranic:root_000516:B004/m01`; reputation and honorable mention `quranic:root_000516:B007/m01`; causing others to hear `quranic:root_000741:B004/m01`; public reputation `quranic:root_000741:B005/m01`; broadcasting a report `quranic:root_000582:B005/m01`; good repute `quranic:root_000890:B002/m01`; good name spreading among people `quranic:root_000745:B008/m01`; talk circulating among people `quranic:root_001272:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 3, 11, 13, 14, 16, 17, and 18.
- Synthesis: Reputation is an outcome of transmission. A tongue originates mention, channels make it audible, broadcast extends its range, and repetition stabilizes it as a public name. This explains why apparently local speech in 49:11-12 can alter standing well beyond the immediate exchange.

#### Subchannel H. A Hostile Name Pushes Kin Away
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An added name creates social distance, turns close kin into outsiders, and can extend disagreement into lasting hostility.
- Active motifs: temporal and relational "after" `quranic:root_000131:B002/m01`; distance from proximity `quranic:root_000131:B001/m01`; making two parties distant `quranic:root_000131:B003/m01`; banishment from good `quranic:root_000131:B004/m01`; distant kin opposed to close relatives `quranic:root_000131:B005/m01`; hostility carried far `quranic:root_000131:B010/m01`; added epithet `quranic:root_001367:B001/m01`; insulting nickname `quranic:root_001467:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: every distance, "after," epithet, and nickname root in this scene occurs at 49:11.
- Synthesis: The phrase "after faith" opens a temporal hinge into a spatial process. A hostile epithet does not remain attached only to a word; it pushes its target away from recognized good and from the proximity of kin. Repeated naming can therefore convert a momentary insult into durable social exile.

#### Subchannel I. The Womb as First Social Container
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Male and female generation passes through a womb, childbirth, and a receiver who brings the newborn into social presence.
- Active motifs: womb as the seedbed and container of offspring `quranic:root_000552:B003/m01`; pain or swelling after birth `quranic:root_000552:B004/m01`; receiver of the newborn `quranic:root_001198:B007/m01`; childbirth and postnatal blood `quranic:root_001533:B005/m01`; male `quranic:root_000516:B001/m01`; female `quranic:root_000058:B001/m01`; creating `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: male, female, creation, and reception/facing occur at 49:13; womb/mercy at 49:5, 10, 12, and 14; self/childbirth at 49:11 and 15.
- Synthesis: The one-origin claim is given an embodied process. Differentiated persons are generated within one containing organ and received into the world through dependent birth. Peoples and tribes therefore begin from shared vulnerability before they become social lines of distinction.

### 9. Value Made Visible in Gift, Work, and Cost
- Semantic invariant: Claims to worth become legible through accountable production, truthful enactment, transfer, and expenditure.
- Surface relation: direct; 49:3 and 14-15 connect reward, undiminished deeds, truth, exertion, wealth, and self, while 49:8 and 17 frame distinction and guidance as bestowed favor.
- Surprising reach: The channel distinguishes spoken claims from manufactured ones, and received favor from the laboring hands and spent capacity by which commitment becomes visible.

#### Subchannel A. Work Receives Its Due
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Intentional work enters an account, creates a due, and receives a return that is neither erased nor cut short.
- Active motifs: wage or recompense `quranic:root_000015:B001/m01`; intentional deed `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01`; wage for work `quranic:root_001046:B004/m01`; doing or producing `quranic:root_001167:B001/m01`; accounting and recompense `quranic:root_000504:B002/m01`; debt or obligation `quranic:root_000504:B003/m01`; cutting off a reward `quranic:root_001449:B001/m01`; diminishing a due `quranic:root_001389:B002/m01`; fulfillment through action `quranic:root_000852:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:2, 3, 6, 14, 15, 16, 17, and 18.
- Synthesis: Value appears as an accountable relation between production and return. Work creates an obligation that can be reckoned, while cutting or diminishing the return violates the due. This supports the assurance of 49:14 without converting inward faith into a merely verbal entitlement.

#### Subchannel B. Laboring Hands Make the Work
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An appointed worker undertakes a task, works it by hand, and receives a wage through a completed transaction.
- Active motifs: wage assigned for work `quranic:root_000248:B005/m01`; appointed worker or office `quranic:root_001046:B003/m01`; wage for work `quranic:root_001046:B004/m01`; work transaction `quranic:root_001046:B005/m01`; manual laborers `quranic:root_001046:B006/m01`; workers who produce `quranic:root_001167:B003/m01`; deeds attributed to hands `quranic:root_001693:B009/m01`; skilled manual craft `quranic:root_001693:B015/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 6, 13, 14, and 18.
- Synthesis: The abstract deed is materialized as assigned labor. Office supplies responsibility, hands supply agency, and transaction links completed work to its wage. The scene pressures status claims by locating visible worth in undertaken work rather than inherited or asserted standing.

#### Subchannel C. Favor Arrives as Gift
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Benefit is handed over as blessing, guidance, distinction, or a gift whose source lies outside the recipient.
- Active motifs: unowed gift or favor `quranic:root_001163:B003/m01`; blessing `quranic:root_001525:B001/m01`; favor placed in another's hand `quranic:root_001693:B003/m01`; guidance as a gift `quranic:root_001583:B004/m01`; gift offered with hope of return `quranic:root_001294:B008/m01`; bestowing good `quranic:root_001449:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: distinction and blessing occur at 49:8; the hand root at 49:1; honor/gift at 49:13; gift-like guidance and bestowal at 49:17.
- Synthesis: Favor is a transfer before it is a possession. Hand, gift, blessing, and guidance identify a giver-recipient relation, while the gift that seeks return reveals how easily transfer can become exchange. The scene keeps received guidance from being redescribed as self-generated merit.

#### Subchannel D. Favor Converted into Social Leverage
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A giver recalls a benefit, burdens the recipient with it, and turns generosity into a claim of superior standing.
- Active motifs: burdening a recipient by reminding them of a gift `quranic:root_001449:B003/m02`; claiming superiority `quranic:root_001163:B004/m01`; outnumbering and boasting `quranic:root_001286:B002/m01`; competing in generosity `quranic:root_001294:B006/m01`; rivalry for name or standing `quranic:root_000745:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:4, 7, 8, 11, 12, 13, 16, 17, and 18.
- Synthesis: This scene reverses the gift relation. What first moved toward another as benefit is pulled back as a debt and used to elevate the giver. The lexical split within the bestowal branch is decisive: the same act can give good or repeatedly impose its memory, which clarifies why claiming credit for surrender is itself a distortion of favor.

#### Subchannel E. A Claim Can Be Spoken or Manufactured
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person articulates a claim, but circulation cannot show whether it reports an inward judgment truthfully or fabricates an attribution.
- Active motifs: articulate self-expression `quranic:root_000996:B001/m01`; spoken utterance `quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`; falsely attributing speech `quranic:root_001272:B005/m01`; unspoken inner saying `quranic:root_001272:B012/m01`; belief or opinion `quranic:root_001272:B013/m01`; invented speech `quranic:root_000434:B007/m01`; manufacturing a falsehood `quranic:root_001167:B004/m01`; truthful speech `quranic:root_000852:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: self-expression occurs at 49:14; saying at 49:2, 14, 16, and 17; creating or inventing at 49:13; making or doing at 49:6; truth at 49:15 and 17.
- Synthesis: Saying is only the visible event. Behind it may stand an inward judgment, while the same productive powers that make deeds can also manufacture speech and attach it falsely to another. The scene sharpens the surah's test of declarations: articulation is evidence that a claim exists, not yet that it is true.

#### Subchannel F. Truth Acquires Solidity in Action
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A truthful declaration becomes firm, complete, and fulfilled when conviction passes into intentional deed.
- Active motifs: truthful speech `quranic:root_000852:B001/m01`; firmness or solidity `quranic:root_000852:B002/m01`; completion and established standing `quranic:root_000852:B003/m01`; fulfillment by action `quranic:root_000852:B004/m01`; belief `quranic:root_000054:B002/m01`; disturbed doubt `quranic:root_000616:B001/m01`; intentional deed `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 15, 17, and 18.
- Synthesis: Truth is more than correspondence between words and facts. Its same-root transformations move from truthful saying to material firmness, completion, and enacted fulfillment. Doubt interrupts that consolidation, while deed gives the declaration a stable form that can bear weight.

#### Subchannel G. Road, Foot, and Intention
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A traveler intends a directed road, puts a foot forward, and advances before the route has been completed.
- Active motifs: road `quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`; traveler `quranic:root_000672:B002/m01`; setting out toward intended work `quranic:root_001046:B011/m01`; intention preceding action `quranic:root_001046:B012/m01`; putting forward or preceding `quranic:root_001207:B001/m01`; foot `quranic:root_001207:B005/m01`; advancing on a matter `quranic:root_001207:B010/m01`; directed exertion `quranic:root_000268:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:1, 2, 14, 15, and 18.
- Synthesis: Commitment has an ordered locomotion: intention points, the foot initiates, and repeated advance carries the agent along a road. This makes the prohibition on advancing in 49:1 structurally relevant to exertion in 49:15: movement is not validated merely by energy, but by its direction and order.

#### Subchannel H. Capacity Spent to Exhaustion
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A traveler crosses hard ground by spending wealth, bodily vigor, and the self until available capacity is depleted.
- Active motifs: utmost exertion under hardship `quranic:root_000268:B001/m01`; hard exposed ground `quranic:root_000268:B004/m01`; exhaustion through use `quranic:root_000268:B005/m01`; wealth `quranic:root_001457:B001/m01`; self or life `quranic:root_001533:B011/m01`; vigor and personal capacity `quranic:root_001533:B014/m01`; hardship `quranic:root_001051:B001/m01`; steep pass `quranic:root_001051:B005/m01`; exhaustion that cuts a traveler off `quranic:root_001449:B001/m02`; supporting strength and its loss `quranic:root_001449:B002/m01`; weak traveler supported between two helpers `quranic:root_001583:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:7, 11, 15, and 17.
- Synthesis: The road's cost is not generic difficulty but consumed capacity. Wealth, vigor, and self are finite resources; exhaustion can cut movement off, while support can keep a weakened traveler moving. Commitment is verified by real expenditure, yet the support motif prevents depletion itself from becoming a solitary badge of rank.

### 10. Moisture, Generosity, and Renewal
- Semantic invariant: Benefit reaches a receptive body as moisture, restores capacity, and becomes visible as fertility, healing, or generosity.
- Surface relation: indirect; 49:4 activates dew and generosity, while mercy, blessing, gift, ground, and the directed way connect the material scene to the surah's forms of favor.
- Surprising reach: The channel models favor by its reviving effect in the recipient rather than by the giver's retained credit.

#### Subchannel A. Rain Reaches Fertile Ground
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Dew and rain descend through the air, reach soft ground, and enable it to bear growth.
- Active motifs: dew `quranic:root_001486:B003/m01`; moisture or dew `quranic:root_000152:B001/m01`; rain falling between cloud and earth `quranic:root_000672:B005/m01`; fertile rain and earth `quranic:root_001294:B002/m01`; soft receptive ground `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: dew occurs at 49:4; road/rain at 49:15; fertile earth and honor at 49:13; earth at 49:16 and 18. Surface anchoring is unavailable for `quranic:root_000152:B001/m01` (`ب ل ل`).
- Synthesis: The scene gives reception a material shape. Moisture must cross a gap and meet ground capable of taking it in; fertility is the resulting state, not a property the rain claims for itself. This usefully reframes guidance and mercy as arrivals whose truth is disclosed by renewed capacity.

#### Subchannel B. Dew Becomes Gift and Healing
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Moisture revives what has dried, passes into generosity, and appears in the recipient as recovery and blessing.
- Active motifs: generosity `quranic:root_001486:B004/m01`; good, gift, or kinship figured as moisture `quranic:root_000152:B002/m01`; recovery or healing `quranic:root_000152:B003/m01`; mercy `quranic:root_000552:B001/m01`; blessing `quranic:root_001525:B001/m01`; unowed gift or favor `quranic:root_001163:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the cited evidence roots occur at 49:4, 5, 8, 10, 12, and 14. Surface anchoring is unavailable for `quranic:root_000152:B002/m01` and `quranic:root_000152:B003/m01` (`ب ل ل`).
- Synthesis: The same lexical family turns wetness into benefit, restored health, and relational good. Mercy and blessing complete the operation: favor matters because it revives and reconnects. This directly resists the conversion of generosity into leverage, since moisture that is hoarded cannot perform the scene.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Calling a Council into Being
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A projected call reaches its hearers, gathers them in one place, and opens negotiation and deliberation.
- Active motifs: council or gathering `quranic:root_001486:B001/m01`; raised call `quranic:root_001486:B002/m01`; calling out `quranic:root_001487:B001/m01`; range reached by a voice `quranic:root_001487:B002/m01`; council `quranic:root_001487:B003/m01`; negotiation through speech `quranic:root_001272:B009/m01`; assembly or place `quranic:root_001273:B006/m01`; counsel and deliberation `quranic:root_000051:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: the call/gathering root occurs at 49:4; counsel/command at 49:7 and 9; saying at 49:2, 14, 16, and 17; standing or gathering at 49:6 and 11. Surface anchoring is unavailable for `quranic:root_001487:B001/m01`, `quranic:root_001487:B002/m01`, and `quranic:root_001487:B003/m01` (`ن د ي`).
- Synthesis: The same call that violates a chamber boundary also has a constructive social operation: it can bring persons within range and form a council. The completed scene is projection, audibility, convergence, negotiation, and deliberation. It remains distinct from voice calibration because its outcome is assembly rather than acoustic hierarchy.
